.

But this configuration is not interference free (because of the location restrictions there is no valid linearization of the read and fulfill events), and we hence (correctly) cannot show outcome
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0,0}\big{(}a=1\wedge b=0\wedge c=1\wedge d=0\big{)}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0,0}\text{ \char 55}}}
to be reachable.

Appendix 0.B
Proofs of Soundness and Completeness

0.B.1
Properties of the Operational Semantics

We start with two properties of the operational semantics. The first ensures that in any trace, the promises can be reordered so that they are executed at the start of the trace without affecting the final outcome.

Lemma 1

For every trace
tr
there exists a trace
tr^{\prime}
(ending in the same final state) such that in
tr^{\prime}
all promise actions of
tr
occur at the beginning followed by the non-promise actions of
tr
(in the same order as in
tr
).

Proof

Let

\langle\overrightarrow{T_{0}},M_{0}\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle\overrightarrow{T_{1}},M_{1}\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{2}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\ldots\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{k-1}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle\overrightarrow{T_{k}},M_{k}\rangle

be a trace
tr
with
I=\{i\mid op_{i}=\mathsf{rd}_{\cdot}(\cdot,\cdot)\}
being the set of all promises.
Let
i_{1}<i_{2}<...
be the elements of
I
.
We now want to swap
op_{i_{1}}
with the operation before it, until
op_{i_{1}}
is the first one in the trace.
After that we do the same with
op_{i_{2}}
until it is the second operation and so on.
For that we need to show that we can swap a promise with a fence, fulfill or read operation before it.

Let
op_{i}
be a promise operation,
l
its location and
op_{i-1}
the operation in front of
op_{i}
.
If we promise something, we change
prom
to
prom\cup\{|M_{i-1}|+1\}
in
ts
and add one element to the memory.
In case that
op_{i-1}
is a fence operation we can reorder
op_{i}
and
op_{i-1}
, because a fence operation changes different attributes of
ts
(
v_{read}
and
v_{write}
) and has no effects on the memory.

If
op_{i-1}
is a fulfill operation, it fulfills a different promise.
Hence the condition

v_{write}\sqcup ts.coh(l)\sqcup v_{a}<t

still applies after the reordering, because
t
depends on the promise
op_{i-1}
is fulfilling.
The changes of
coh
and
v_{write}
are not related to the changes caused by
op_{i}
.
Additionally the element deleted by
op_{i-1}
in
prom
is not the same as the element that
op_{i}
adds.
So we can also reorder a promise and a fulfill.

In case that
op_{i-1}
is a read operation,
op_{i-1}
changes other attributes of
ts
then
op_{i}
would.
If
op_{i-1}
reads from a location
l^{\prime}
, which is different from
l
, the condition

\forall t^{\prime}.\ t<t^{\prime}\leq(ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.coh(l))\Rightarrow M(t^{\prime}).loc\neq l

holds.
However, when
op_{i-1}
reads from
l
, we need to have a deeper look at
v_{read}
and
coh
.
Both will only be replaced by timestamps of entries in
M_{i-1}
.
Those timestamps are always smaller then
|M_{i-1}|+1
, so the condition is still true.
Hence we can reorder a read and a promise operation.

That means we can reorder the operations of our execution in a way that all promises are at the beginning and the final state stays the same.

The second property ensures that views of each component monotonically increase after each transition. Note that this differs from the semantics of Pulte et al., where register views might decrease after the execution of a write to a local register
[
31
]
.

Lemma 2
(View monotonicity)

Suppose

\langle\langle S,ts\rangle,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\langle\langle S^{\prime},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M^{\prime}\rangle
. Then for
view\in\{v_{read},v_{wNew},v_{wOld},v_{C}\}
, we have

ts.view\leq ts^{\prime}.view
. Moreover, for all locations
x
, we have
ts.coh(x)\leq ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
, and for all registers
a
, we have that if
ts.regs(a)=\kappa@v
and
ts^{\prime}.regs(a)=\kappa^{\prime}@v^{\prime}
, then
v\leq v^{\prime}
.

Proof

Let
\langle\langle S,ts\rangle,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\langle\langle S^{\prime},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M^{\prime}\rangle
.

•

v_{C}
only changes for
op=asm(bexp)
:
ts^{\prime}.v_{C}=ts.v_{C}\sqcup ts.v\geq ts.v_{C}

•

v_{wOld}
only changes for
op={\it ff}(x,\kappa)
:
ts^{\prime}.v_{wOld}=ts.v_{wOld}\sqcup t\geq ts.v_{wOld}

•

coh(x)
changes in two cases:

–

for
op=rd(x,\kappa)
:
ts^{\prime}.coh(x)=ts.coh(x)\sqcup v_{post}\geq ts.coh(x)

–

for
op={\it ff}(x,\kappa)
:
ts^{\prime}.coh(x)=t>ts.coh(x)
because
ts.v_{wNew}\sqcup ts.v_{C}\sqcup ts.coh(x)\sqcup v_{a}<t

•

v_{read}
changes in two cases:

–

for
op=rd(x,\kappa)
:
ts^{\prime}.v_{read}=v_{post}=ts.v_{read}\sqcup t\geq ts.v_{read}

–

for
op=\mathsf{fnc}
:
ts^{\prime}.v_{read}=ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.v_{wOld}\geq ts.v_{read}

•

v_{wNew}
only changes for
op=\mathsf{fnc}
:
ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}=ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.v_{wOld}\geq ts.v_{read}
. By looking at
v_{read}
we see that
ts^{\prime}.v_{read}\geq ts.v_{read}
holds in all cases of
op
. Since
v_{read}
changes whenever
v_{wNew}
does and both are set to the same value we get
ts.v_{read}\geq ts.v_{wNew}
. Therefore
ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}\geq ts.v_{wNew}
.

•

For
regs(a)=\kappa@v_{a}
we first show that
ts.v_{a}\leq ts.v_{read}
is an invariant. We know that
v_{a}
only changes for
op=rd(x,\kappa)
and
op=\mathit{lst}(a,exp)
whereas
v_{read}
changes for
op=rd(x,\kappa)
and
op=\mathsf{fnc}
. For
op=rd(x,\kappa)

v_{a}
and
v_{read}
are set to the same value. Since
ts^{\prime}.v_{read}\geq ts.v_{read}
,
v_{a}
can not get bigger than
v_{read}
after a register operation. Therefore our invariant holds and we get:

–

for
op=rd(x,\kappa)
:
ts^{\prime}.v_{a}=ts.v_{read}\sqcup t\geq ts.v_{read}\geq ts.v_{a}

–

since
op=\mathit{lst}(a,exp)
changes
v_{a}
to the maximum of
v_{a}
and the view of
exp
.,
ts^{\prime}.v_{a}\geq ts.v_{a}

0.B.2
Soundness

Next we prove soundness of our proof rules. We first prove correctness of each of the atomic rules from
Fig.

7
.

Proposition 1
(Soundness of rule
PR-WriteC
)

Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)\rrbracket
. Then there exists
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{store}}\,\,x\,\,\kappa,ts\rangle,M\big{\rangle}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{skip}},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M\big{\rangle}
.

Proof

We write
{\cal E}^{\prime}
for
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
. Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}^{\prime}\rrbracket
and let
e^{\prime}
be the event such that
\ell(e^{\prime})=\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)

This means that there exists some mapping
\psi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}^{\prime})\rightarrow dom(M)
that shows
\langle ts,M\rangle
satisfies
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.
In particular,
\psi^{\prime}(e^{\prime})=t
implies that
M(t)=\langle x:=\kappa\rangle_{\tau}
.
We show that there exists some
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that the following holds (eliding the program statements):

\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle

Note that
M
is unchanged and
t
in the rule
Fulfill
is instantiated to
ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
. We define
ts
to be the following by using
\psi^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle ts.prom

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.prom\cup\{ts^{\prime}.coh(x)\}

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle ts.v_{a}

\displaystyle:=0

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{read}

\displaystyle ts.v_{C}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{C}

First, we show
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
by checking the conditions in
Section

5.1
.

1.

We define
\psi=\{e^{\prime}\}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 0.21529pt\hbox{$-$}\hss}{\lhd}}\psi^{\prime}
, where
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 0.21529pt\hbox{$-$}\hss}{\lhd}}
denotes domain anti-restriction. Clearly
M
remains consistent with
{\cal E}
.

2.

This is trivial. We have one additional open promise in
ts.prom
, but this is precisely the fulfilled write in
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.

3.

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{C}

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{C}

by definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

unfolding definition of
ts^{\prime}.v_{C}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})\subseteq dom(\psi)

•

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})\subseteq dom(\psi)

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\subseteq dom(\psi)

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}

by definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\big{(}\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}}\psi(e)

unfolding definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\big{(}\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}}\psi(e)

since
\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\big{(}\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})\big{)}}\psi(e)

logic

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}

\displaystyle=ts.v_{read}

by definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)

unfolding

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

since
\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})

and
\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\big{)}\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

logic

4.

Trivial since no register value is modified.

Recall:

\displaystyle{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(E\cup\{e\},\mathord{\twoheadrightarrow}\cup\{(e^{\prime},e)\mid e^{\prime}\in last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}\cup\{\mathsf{fnc}_{\tau},\mathsf{tst}_{\tau}(\cdot)\}}({\cal E}),\Lambda,\ell[e\mapsto\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa))])

Next, we have
\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle
as follows:

•

ts^{\prime}.prom=ts.prom\backslash\{t\}
by definition

•

regs(\kappa)=\kappa@0
because
ts.v_{a}=ts^{\prime}.v_{a}
and
ts.regs(a)=ts^{\prime}.regs(a)
,

•

M(t)=\langle x:=\kappa\rangle_{\tau}
by definition

•

ts.v_{wNew}\sqcup ts.v_{C}\sqcup ts.coh(x)\sqcup ts.v_{a}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
. We prove maximality of
ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
for each component:

–

ts.v_{wNew}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)

Recall that

ts.v_{wNew}=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap(\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}))}\psi(e)
.
By definition of
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
, we have that the event corresponding to
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
is causally after each
e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})
. Thus,
ts.v_{wNew}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
.

–

ts.v_{C}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
.

Recall that
ts.v_{C}=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)
. By definition of
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
, we have that the event corresponding to
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
is causally after each
e\in\mathit{\mathit{prTst}}_{\tau}({\cal E})
. Thus
ts.v_{C}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
.

–

ts.coh(x)<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
because by definition of
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
we have a causal edge from each
e\in Act^{x}
to the event corresponding to
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
.

–

ts.v_{a}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
because
v_{a}=v_{\kappa}=0
.

Proposition 2
(Soundness of rule
PR-WriteR
)

Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)\rrbracket
and
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\cal E}=\kappa
. Then there exists
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{store}}\,\,x\,\,a,ts\rangle,M\big{\rangle}

\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{skip}},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M\big{\rangle}
.

Proof

We write
{\cal E}^{\prime}
for
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
. Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}^{\prime}\rrbracket
and let
e^{\prime}
be the event such that
\ell(e^{\prime})=\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)

This means that there exists some mapping
\psi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}^{\prime})\rightarrow dom(M)
that shows
\langle ts,M\rangle
satisfies
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.
In particular,
\psi^{\prime}(e^{\prime})=t
implies that
M(t)=\langle x:=\kappa\rangle_{\tau}
.
We show that there exists some
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that the following holds (eliding the program statements):

\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle

Note that
M
is unchanged and
t
in the rule
Fulfill
is instantiated to
ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
. We define
ts
to be the following by using
\psi^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle ts.prom

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.prom\cup\{ts^{\prime}.coh(x)\}

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle ts.v_{a}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{a}

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{read}

\displaystyle ts.v_{C}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{C}

First, we show
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
by checking the conditions in
Section

5.1
.

1.

We define
\psi=\{e^{\prime}\}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 0.21529pt\hbox{$-$}\hss}{\lhd}}\psi^{\prime}
, where
\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\raise 0.21529pt\hbox{$-$}\hss}{\lhd}}
denotes domain anti-restriction. Clearly
M
remains consistent with
{\cal E}
.

2.

This is trivial. We have one additional open promise in
ts.prom
, but this is precisely the fulfilled write in
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.

3.

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{C}

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{C}

by definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

unfolding definition of
ts^{\prime}.v_{C}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})\subseteq dom(\psi)

•

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})\subseteq dom(\psi)

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\subseteq dom(\psi)

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}

by definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\big{(}\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}}\psi(e)

unfolding definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\big{(}\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}}\psi(e)

since
\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\big{(}\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})\big{)}}\psi(e)

logic

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{a}

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{a}

by definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{a}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)

unfolding definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{a}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

since
\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{a}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{a}({\cal E})

•

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}

\displaystyle=ts.v_{read}

by definition

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)

unfolding

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\big{)}\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

since
\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})

and
\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\big{)}\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

logic

4.

Trivial since no register value is modified.

Recall:

\displaystyle{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(E\cup\{e\},\mathord{\twoheadrightarrow}\cup\{(e^{\prime},e)\mid e^{\prime}\in last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}\cup\{\mathsf{fnc}_{\tau},\mathsf{tst}_{\tau}(\cdot),\mathsf{bar}(a,\cdot)\}}({\cal E}),\Lambda,\ell[e\mapsto\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa))])

Next, we have
\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle
as follows:

•

ts^{\prime}.prom=ts.prom\backslash\{t\}
by definition

•

regs(a)=\kappa@v_{a}
because
ts.v_{a}=ts^{\prime}.v_{a}
and
ts.regs(a)=ts^{\prime}.regs(a)
,

•

M(t)=\langle x:=\kappa\rangle_{\tau}
by definition

•

ts.v_{wNew}\sqcup ts.v_{C}\sqcup ts.coh(x)\sqcup ts.v_{a}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
. We prove maximality of
ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
for each component:

–

ts.v_{wNew}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)

Recall that

ts.v_{wNew}=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap(\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}))}\psi(e)
.
By definition of
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
, we have that the event corresponding to
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,n)
is causally after each
e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})
. Thus,
ts.v_{wNew}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
.

–

ts.v_{C}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
.

Recall that
ts.v_{C}=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)
. By definition of
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
, we have that the event corresponding to
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
is causally after each
e\in\mathit{\mathit{prTst}}_{\tau}({\cal E})
. Thus
ts.v_{C}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
.

–

ts.coh(x)<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
because by definition of
{\cal E}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
we have a causal edge from each
e\in Act^{x}
to the event corresponding to
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
.

–

ts.v_{a}<ts^{\prime}.coh(x)
same
Because

\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{r}({\cal E})}\psi(e)=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{r}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)

Moreover
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
goes at the end and
\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{r}({\cal E})=\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{r}({\cal E}^{\prime})

Proposition 3
(Soundness of rule
PR-ReadEx
)

Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket\mathit{rstr}^{x}_{e}({\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,x))\rrbracket
, where
e
is the event in rule
PR-ReadEx
. Then there exists
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\big{\langle}\langle a:=\textnormal{{load}}\,x,ts\rangle,M\big{\rangle}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle rd(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{skip}},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M\big{\rangle}
.

Proof

For the proof, we elide the program statements. We write
{\cal E}^{\prime}
for
\mathit{rstr}_{e}^{x}({\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,x))
. Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
This means that there exists some mapping
\psi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\rightarrow dom(M)
such that
\psi^{\prime}
can be used to show that
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle
satisfies
{\cal E}^{\prime}
, in particular by preservation of memory constraints for the location-restricted red flows for all timestamps
t^{\prime}
, events
f
such that
\psi^{\prime}(e)<t^{\prime}<\psi^{\prime}(f)
and
e\twoheadrightarrow f
and
f\in(\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E}^{\prime})
we have
M(t^{\prime}).loc\neq e.loc
. We now show that there exists some
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle rd(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle

Note that
M
stays the same as we do not have any further promises. We define
ts
to be the following (using
\psi:=\psi^{\prime}
):

\displaystyle ts.prom

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.prom

\displaystyle ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle ts.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle ts.v_{b}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{b}\qquad(b\neq a)

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}^{x}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{a}

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{C}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{C}

Since
{\cal E}^{\prime}
neither has additional fulfill nor read events nor fences nor tests and
M
is the same and
\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})=\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})
, we by definition get
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
.
Next, we need to determine the timestamp
t
for reading, which in this case is
\psi(e)
. Since
\psi
preserves contents and
\ell(e)\in\{\mathsf{rd}_{\tau}(x,\kappa),\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa),\mathsf{ini}\}
, we get
M(t).loc=x
and
M(t).val=\kappa
(
\kappa=0
in case of reading initial values). Now consider some timestamp
t^{\prime}
such that
t<t^{\prime}\leq ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.coh(x)
.
Let
f
be the event such that
\psi(f)=ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.coh(x)
. This implies (definition of
ts.coh(x)
and
ts_{v_{read}}
) that
f\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}^{x}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup(\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})
. Thus we also get
f\in(\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E}^{\prime})
. By preservation of memory constraints we thus have
M(t^{\prime}).loc\neq e.loc
.

Next to the views. We need to show the correct afterstate for three views being changed by the read rule:
v_{a},coh(x)
and
v_{read}
.

•

v_{a}
is set to
ts.v_{read}\sqcup t
. We get

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}\sqcup t

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup t

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{a}

as
\oplus
in particular introduces a flow between the event read from (which is labelled with some action in
\mathit{Act}^{x}
) and other bar events to the event
\mathsf{bar}(a,x)
.

•

coh(x)
is set to
coh(x)\sqcup v_{read}\sqcup t
. We get

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)\sqcup ts.v_{read}\sqcup t

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}^{x}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup t

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup t

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}^{x}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.coh(x)

This holds as the new bar event is flow after all other bars and all fences (by definition of
\oplus
).

•

v_{read}
is set to
v_{read}\sqcup t
. We get

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}\sqcup t

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup t

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{read}

•

Finally, we look at the value in register
a
which is set to
\kappa
(which is
M(t).val
).
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}=\kappa
as the reading event
e
is in
last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E}^{\prime})
(as this is equal to
last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E})
and reading from last events is required by the hypothesis of rule
PR-ReadEx
) and the event is in
\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E}^{\prime})
(by definition of
\oplus
).

Proposition 4
(Soundness of rule
PR-ReadNew
)

Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket({\cal E}\oplus{\cal E}^{\prime})\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,x)\rrbracket
,
{\cal E}^{\prime}
as of rule
PR-ReadNew
. Then there exists
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that
\big{\langle}\langle a:=\textnormal{{load}}\,x,ts\rangle,M\big{\rangle}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle rd(x,\kappa)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{skip}},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M\big{\rangle}
.

Proof

For the proof, we elide the program statements. We write
{\cal E}_{\mathit{after}}
for
({\cal E}\oplus{\cal E}^{\prime})\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,x)
. Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{\mathit{after}}\rrbracket
.
This means that there exists some mapping
\psi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}_{\mathit{after}})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}_{\mathit{after}})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\rightarrow dom(M)
such that
\psi^{\prime}
can be used to show that
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle
satisfies
{\cal E}_{\mathit{after}}
. Let
e
be the event in
{\cal E}^{\prime}
which we read from and let
t=\psi^{\prime}(e)
. We set
ts
to be the thread state which we construct from
{\cal E}
and
M
using
\psi
for which we take
\psi^{\prime}|_{\cal E}
. By definition of
{\cal E}_{\mathit{after}}
,
e
is flow-after all fulfills of
x
, all fences plus reads in
{\cal E}
which are followed by a bar event (and
e
not in
{\cal E}
). As
\psi^{\prime}
is preserving flows, all events
f\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{prBar}({\cal E})
(which determine
ts.v_{read}
) have a timestamp less than
t
, hence
ts.v_{read}<t
. Similarly, all events
f\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{prBar}^{x}_{\tau}({\cal E})
(which determine
ts.coh(x)
) have a timestamp
less than
t
and hence
ts.coh(x)<t
. Hence the condition of the read rule in the operational semantics about locations is trivially fulfilled. Moreover,
v_{post}
is
t
. Thus,
v_{a}
as well as
v_{read}
and
coh(x)
are set to
t
, and this then coincides with
ts^{\prime}.v_{a}
, (as
ts^{\prime}.v_{a}=\bigsqcup_{f\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E}_{after})}\psi^{\prime}(f)
and
e
is the maximal event in this set), with
ts^{\prime}.v_{read}
(similarly, because
e
is the maximal event in
(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}_{after})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}_{after}))\cup\mathit{prBar}({\cal E}_{after})
and with
ts^{\prime}.coh(x)

(similarly, because
e
is the maximal event in
\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E}_{after})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E}_{after})
), respectively. Views
v_{wOld}
,
v_{wNew}
and
v_{C}
keep their values as of the operational semantics, and indeed calculating their views from
{\cal E}
and
{\cal E}_{after}
gives the same value as we neither introduce new test events (this would change
v_{C}
) nor new fulfills (would change
v_{wOld}
) nor fences (would change
v_{wNew}
).

Finally, we look at the value in register
a
which is set to
\kappa
(which is
M(t).val
).
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}_{\mathit{after}}}=\kappa
as the reading event
e
with label
\mathsf{rd}_{\tau^{\prime}}(x,\kappa)
is in
last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E}_{\mathit{after}})\cap\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E}_{\mathit{after}})
.

Proposition 5
(Soundness of rule
PR-Fence
)

Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{fnc}_{\tau}\rrbracket
. Then there exists
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that
\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{dmb}},ts\rangle,M\big{\rangle}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{fnc}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{skip}},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M\big{\rangle}
.

Proof

For the proof, we elide the program statements. We write
{\cal E}^{\prime}
for
{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{fnc}_{\tau}
. Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
This means that there exists some mapping
\psi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\rightarrow dom(M)
such that
\psi^{\prime}
can be used to show that
\langle ts,M\rangle
satisfies
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.
We now show that there exists some
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{fnc}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle

Note that
M
stays the same as we do not have any further promises. We define
ts
to be the following by using
\psi:=\psi^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle ts.prom

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.prom

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.coh(x)

\displaystyle ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle ts.v_{r}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{r}

\displaystyle ts.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap(\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E}))}\psi(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{\mathit{prFnc}}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{\mathit{prBar}}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

Since
{\cal E}^{\prime}
neither has additional fulfill, read nor bar events,
M
is the same and
\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})=\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})
is the same, we by definition get
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
. Next to the views. We need to show the correct afterstate for two views being changed by the fence rule:
v_{wNew}
and
v_{read}
. Both are set to
ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.v_{wOld}
.

•

For
v_{read}
we get

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{read}

as
\oplus\mathsf{fnc}_{\tau}
introduces a flow from all last events to the new fence and so
\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})=\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})
.

•

For
v_{wNew}
we get

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}\sqcup ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cap(\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime}))}\psi^{\prime}(e)

\displaystyle=ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}

as
\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})
are subsets of
\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})
.

Proposition 6
(Soundness of rule
PR-Register
)

Suppose that
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,exp)\rrbracket
. Then there exists
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\big{\langle}\langle a:=exp,ts\rangle,M\big{\rangle}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathit{lst}(a,exp)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{skip}},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M\big{\rangle}
.

Proof

For the proof, we elide the program statements. We write
{\cal E}^{\prime}
for
{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,exp)
. Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
This means that there exists some mapping
\psi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Ini}\rightarrow dom(M)
such that
\psi^{\prime}
can be used to show that
\langle ts,M\rangle
satisfies
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.
We now show that there exists some
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathit{lst}(a,exp)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle

Note that
M
stays the same as we do not have any further promises. We define
ts
to be the following by using
\psi:=\psi^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle ts.prom

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.prom

\displaystyle ts.coh(x)

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.coh(x)

\displaystyle ts.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle ts.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{wNew}

\displaystyle ts.v_{a}

\displaystyle:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle ts.v_{read}

\displaystyle:=ts^{\prime}.v_{read}

We need to show the correct afterstate for
v_{a}
and
reg(a)
being changed by the register rule.

•

For
v_{a}
we get

\displaystyle ts.v_{a}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{a^{\prime}\in R(exp)}ts.v_{a^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{a^{\prime}\in R(exp)}\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a^{\prime}}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E})\cup\bigcup_{a^{\prime}\in R(exp)}\mathit{prBar}_{a^{\prime}}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi^{\prime}(e)

as
\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,exp)
introduces for every
b\in R(exp)\cup\{a\}
a flow from the last
\mathsf{bar}(b,\cdot)
to
\mathsf{bar}(a,exp)
and so
\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E}^{\prime})=\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E})\cup\bigcup_{a^{\prime}\in R(exp)}\mathit{prBar}_{a^{\prime}}({\cal E})
.

•

For
reg(a)
we need to show
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}=\kappa
with
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{reg}=\kappa@v
. Let
exp=e_{1}op_{1}\ldots op_{k-1}e_{k}
with
\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{reg}=\kappa_{i}@v_{i}
. We get

\displaystyle\llbracket a\rrbracket_{reg}

\displaystyle=\llbracket exp\rrbracket_{reg}

\displaystyle=\llbracket e_{1}op_{1}\ldots op_{k-1}e_{k}\rrbracket_{reg}

\displaystyle=(\kappa_{1}\llbracket op_{1}\rrbracket\ldots\llbracket op_{k-1}\rrbracket\kappa_{k})@(v_{1}\sqcup\ldots\sqcup v_{k})

and

\displaystyle\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket exp\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket e_{1}op_{1}\ldots op_{k-1}e_{k}\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket e_{1}\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}\llbracket op_{1}\rrbracket\ldots\llbracket op_{k-1}\rrbracket\llbracket e_{1}\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\llbracket op_{1}\rrbracket\ldots\llbracket op_{k-1}\rrbracket\kappa_{k}

as
\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{{\cal E}^{\prime}}=\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{{\cal E}}
and
\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{{\cal E}}=\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{reg}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.

Proposition 7
(Soundness of rule
PR-Assume
)

Suppose that
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{tst}_{\tau}(bexp)\rrbracket
such that
\llbracket bexp\rrbracket_{\cal E}=\mathsf{true}
. Then there exists
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that
\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{assume}}\ bexp,ts\rangle,M\big{\rangle}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle asm(bexp))}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\big{\langle}\langle\textnormal{{skip}},ts^{\prime}\rangle,M\big{\rangle}
.

Proof

For the proof, we elide the program statements. We write
{\cal E}^{\prime}
for
{\cal E}\oplus\mathsf{tst}_{\tau}(bexp)
. Let
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
This means that there exists some mapping
\psi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}^{\prime})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\rightarrow dom(M)
such that
\psi^{\prime}
can be used to show that
\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle
satisfies
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.
We now show that there exists some
\langle ts,M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
such that

\langle ts,M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{tst}_{\tau}(bexp)}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau}\langle ts^{\prime},M\rangle

Note that
M
stays the same as we do not have any further promises. We define
ts
to be the following by using
\psi:=\psi^{\prime}
: We take
ts.v:=ts^{\prime}.v
for all views except for
v_{C}
. For the control dependence view
v_{C}
we take
ts.v_{C}:=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)
. Moreover, we let
ts.regs(a)=\kappa_{a}@ts.v_{a}
where
\kappa_{a}=\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\cal E}
.
Note that
ts\in\llbracket{\cal E}\rrbracket
.
Furthermore, since
\llbracket bexp\rrbracket_{\cal E}=\mathsf{true}
(precondition of proof rule for assume), we get
\llbracket bexp\rrbracket_{ts.regs}=\mathsf{true}@v
for
v=v_{a_{1}}\sqcup\ldots\sqcup v_{a_{m}}
such that
R(bexp)=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\}
.
Hence the transition
asm(bexp)
is enabled in
ts
. It remains to be shown that we reach
ts^{\prime}
by taking this transition. The interesting case is
v_{C}
:

\displaystyle ts.v_{C}\sqcup v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup v_{a_{1}}\sqcup\ldots v_{a_{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a_{1}}({\cal E})}\psi(e)\sqcup\ldots\sqcup\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a_{m}}({\cal E})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{\prime})}\psi(e)

In addition, we need to (define and) show the soundness of the proof rule
Parallel
. This is a bit more complex.
We start with two propositions on the existence of memories and on thread states belonging to combined and individual event structures.

Proposition 8

Let
{\cal E}_{1},\ldots,{\cal E}_{n}
be local assertions of threads
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{n}
, respectively. If
{\cal E}_{1},\ldots,{\cal E}_{n}
are synchronizable and
C
is an interference free configuration of
\mathit{Conf}({\cal E}_{1}||\ldots||{\cal E}_{n})
, then there exists some memory
M
and thread states
ts_{1},\ldots,ts_{n}
(of threads
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{n}
, respectively) such that
\langle ts_{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{C}\rrbracket
,
i=1,\ldots,n
.

Proof

Let
C
be an interference free configuration of
{\cal E}_{1}||\ldots||{\cal E}_{n}
. Let
\prec
be the total memory consistent linearizable order. We assume it to be
e_{\mathsf{ini}}\prec e_{1}\prec\ldots\prec e_{m}
for
E_{C}=\{e_{\mathsf{ini}},e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}\}
. We now construct the following memory
M
:

\displaystyle M(0)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{ini}

\displaystyle M(i)

\displaystyle=\langle x,\kappa\rangle_{\tau}\quad\text{ if }\ell(e_{i})\in\{\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}(x,\kappa),\mathsf{rd}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)\}

With this, we let
\psi
map
e_{\mathsf{ini}}
to 0 and
e_{i}
to
i
. By construction and
C
being interference free,
\psi
is (i) initializing at zero, (ii) consecutive, (iii) content preserving, (iv) flow preserving, and (v) memory-constraints preserving. The thread states
ts_{1},\ldots,ts_{n}
can then simply be calculated from
M
,
\psi
and
C
.

The second proposition is a sort of compositionality result of parallel composition with respect to the global state (views and memory) of a program and its projection onto the threads and their individual proof outlines.

Proposition 9

If
{\cal E}_{1}
to
{\cal E}_{n}
are synchronizable and
C
is an interference free configuration of
\mathit{Conf}({\cal E}_{1}||\ldots||{\cal E}_{n})
, then
\langle ts_{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{C}\rrbracket
implies
\langle ts_{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{i}\rrbracket
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.

Proof

Let
\langle ts_{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{C}\rrbracket
.
Then there exists a mapping
\psi:\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}_{C})\cup\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}_{C})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\rightarrow dom(M)
.
Out of
\psi
, we construct
\psi_{i}:\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}_{i})\cup\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}_{i})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\rightarrow dom(M)
by letting

\displaystyle\psi_{i}:\quad

\displaystyle e_{\mathsf{ini}}\mapsto 0

\displaystyle e_{i}\mapsto t

\displaystyle\quad\text{ if }\exists(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})\in E_{C}:\psi(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})=t

We have to show that this
\psi_{i}
is well defined and total.

•

it is total, because
C
is thread-covering,

•

well defined:

assume exists another, different event

(e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n}^{\prime})\in E_{C}
. Since

e_{i}\neq*
, we have (by the definition of parallel
composition) that

(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})\#(e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n}^{\prime})

and then by definition of configuration we
cannot have both events in
C
.

We further need to show that
\psi_{i}
satisfies the conditions (i) to (v) of
\llbracket{\cal E}_{i}\rrbracket
.

1.

it initializes at zero: by construction,

2.

consecutive for
\tau_{i}
: follows because
\psi
is consecutive,

3.

content preserving: by construction,

4.

flow order preserving:
let
e_{i}\twoheadrightarrow^{+}_{i}d_{i}
. Because
C
is thread-covering, there exist events
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n}),

(d_{1},\ldots,d_{i},\ldots,d_{n})\in E_{C}
such that

(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})\twoheadrightarrow^{+}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{i},\ldots,d_{n}).

Hence
\psi(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})<\psi(d_{1},\ldots,d_{i},\ldots,d_{n})
which implies
\psi_{i}(e_{i})<\psi_{i}(d_{i})
by construction.

5.

memory constraints preserving: let
d_{i}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}}}_{i}e_{i}
, then there exists

(d_{1},\ldots,d_{i},\ldots,d_{n})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0,0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L^{\prime}}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}}}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})

for some
L^{\prime}\supseteq L
. Hence for all
t
such that
\psi(d_{1},\ldots,d_{i},\ldots,d_{n})<t<\psi(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})
we get
M(t).loc\neq(d_{1},\ldots,d_{i},\ldots,d_{n}).loc
. Hence we also have
\forall t:\psi_{i}(d_{i})<t<\psi_{i}(e_{i})
:
M(t).loc\neq d_{i}.loc
.

We further need to show that the promises are ok:

We have
ts_{i}.prom=M_{\tau_{i}}\setminus\psi(\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau_{i}}({\cal E}_{C}))
. By the definition of labelling in parallel composition and the fact that all
{\cal E}_{j}
,
j\neq i
, contain no events labelled
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{i}}(\cdot,\cdot)
1
1
1
This holds because the proof rules only introduce fulfill events of a thread
\tau
in the proof outline of
\tau
.
, we get that for all
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})\in E_{C}
s.t.
\ell(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})=\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{1}}(\cdot,\cdot)
we have
\ell_{i}(e_{i})=\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{i}}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Furthermore
\psi(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i},\ldots,e_{n})=\psi_{i}(e_{i})
. Hence
M_{\tau_{i}}\setminus\psi(\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau_{i}}({\cal E}_{C}))=M_{\tau_{i}}\setminus\psi_{i}(\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau_{i}}({\cal E}_{i}))
.

Next to the views:

First
v_{C}
. We have

ts_{i}.v_{C}=\bigsqcup_{(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau_{i}}({\cal E}_{C})}\psi(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}).

Recall that
\mathit{prTst}_{\tau_{i}}({\cal E}_{C})=\{(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\in\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}_{C})\cup\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}_{C})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\mid\exists(e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{n}^{\prime})\in last_{\mathsf{tst}_{\tau_{i}}(\cdot)}({\cal E}_{C}):(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\twoheadrightarrow^{+}(e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{n}^{\prime})\}
.
First, note that
e_{j}^{\prime}=*
,
j\neq i
(since this is a test-labelled event which is not synchronized).
Second, note that we do not necessarily have
e_{i}\twoheadrightarrow_{i}^{+}e_{i}^{\prime}
. However, there exists a sequence
(e_{1}^{1},\ldots,e_{n}^{1}),\ldots,(e_{1}^{m},\ldots,e_{n}^{m})
such that

(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})=(e_{1}^{1},\ldots,e_{n}^{1})\twoheadrightarrow\ldots\twoheadrightarrow(e_{1}^{n},\ldots,e_{n}^{m})=(e_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,e_{n}^{\prime})

with some
j,1\leq j\leq m-1
s.t.
(e_{1}^{j},\ldots,e_{n}^{j})\in sync({\cal E}_{1},\ldots,{\cal E}_{n})
and

e_{i}^{j}\twoheadrightarrow_{1}\ldots\twoheadrightarrow_{1}e_{i}^{n}\ .

We furthermore have
\psi(e_{1}^{j},\ldots,e_{n}^{j})=\psi_{i}(e_{i}^{j})
and
\psi(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})<\psi(e_{1}^{j},\ldots,e_{n}^{j})
(by
\psi
being flow preserving).
Thus
\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau_{i}}({\cal E}_{C})}\psi(e)=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau_{i}}({\cal E}_{i})}\psi_{i}(e)
.

The other views have a similar reasoning, because
\mathit{prBar}
and
\mathit{prFnc}
are also sets of events prior to an event of the form
(*,\ldots,*,\cdot,*,\ldots,*)
. Moreover, if
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\in\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}_{C})
, then
e_{i}\in\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}_{i})
.

Finally, the register values. We need to show that
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}_{C}}=\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}_{i}}
for all registers
a\in R(\tau_{i})
. Recall that
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}_{C}}
is calculated via
\mathit{prBar}_{a}
. As
a
is local to
\tau_{i}
, an ordering with respect to a
\mathsf{bar}(a,\cdot)
-labelled event has to come from the event structure
{\cal E}_{i}
. Hence, the same value is calculated in
{\cal E}_{C}
and
{\cal E}_{i}
.

These two propositions next help us to establish our first main result which is the soundness of the rule of parallel composition.

See
5.1

Proof

Note first that by Proposition
8
such states
\langle ts_{i},M\rangle
always exist for interference free configurations.

For the proof, we basically show the existence of an execution of this form:

\langle\vec{T}_{0},M_{0}\rangle\underbrace{\longrightarrow^{*}}_{\text{all promises}}\langle\vec{T}_{0}^{p},M\rangle\underbrace{\longrightarrow^{*}}_{\text{the statements}}\langle\vec{T},M\rangle

First note, that
Lemma

1
tells us that such a trace which first executes all promises and then the remaining operations exists. We first show
\langle\vec{T}_{0},M_{0}\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}^{*}\langle\vec{T}_{0}^{p},M\rangle
.
For this, we let
\vec{T}_{0}^{p}
be the thread pool coinciding with
\vec{T}_{0}
except for the promise sets which are
ts_{i}.prom=M_{\tau_{i}}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Assume
\#M=m
,
M(j)=\langle x:=\kappa\rangle_{\tau}
and let
op_{j}=prm_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
,
1\leq j\leq m
. Then

\langle\vec{T}_{0},M_{0}\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}\ldots op_{m}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle\vec{T}_{0}^{p},M\rangle

(promises are made in the order as given by
M
) and all these steps are certified as the promises will finally be empty.

Next, we consider the steps of statements. For this, first let
ts_{i}^{p}
be
\vec{T}_{0}^{p}(\tau_{i}).tstate
be the state of thread
\tau_{i}
after having made the promises. Let
op_{1}^{i}\ldots op_{k_{i}}^{i}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, and
op_{j}^{i}\neq\mathsf{rd}_{\tau_{i}}(\cdot,\cdot)
, be the sequence of non-promise operations such that
S_{i}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}^{i}\ldots op_{k_{i}}^{i}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau_{i}}\textnormal{{skip}}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
From
\langle ts_{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{i}\rrbracket
and Prop.
9
, we know that
\langle ts_{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{i}\rrbracket
.
By soundness of local proof rules, we get the existence of thread states
ts_{i}^{0},\ldots,ts_{i}^{k_{i}}
for all threads such that

\langle ts_{i}^{p},M\rangle=\langle ts_{i}^{0},M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}^{i}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau_{i}}\ldots\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{k_{i}}^{i}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau_{i}}\langle ts_{i}^{k_{i}},M\rangle=\langle ts_{i},M\rangle

These steps now have to be lifted to program steps of the parallel program. We construct a sequence of thread pools
\vec{T}_{1}^{0},\ldots,\vec{T}_{1}^{k_{1}}
,
\ldots
,
\vec{T}_{n}^{1},\ldots,\vec{T}_{n}^{k_{n}}
sequentially executing all thread programs by letting (1)
\vec{T}_{\ell}^{j}(\tau_{i})=(S_{i},ts_{i}^{p})
for
\ell<i
, (2)
\vec{T}_{\ell}^{j}(\tau_{i})=(\textnormal{{skip}},ts_{i})
for
\ell>i
, and (3)
\vec{T}_{\ell}^{j}(\tau_{i})=(S_{i}^{\prime},ts_{i}^{j})
for
\ell=i
for some
S_{i}^{\prime}
such that
S_{i}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{i}^{1}\ldots op_{j}^{i}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau_{i}}S_{i}^{\prime}
.
Note that
\vec{T}_{0}^{p}=\vec{T}_{1}^{0}
and
\vec{T}=\vec{T_{n}}^{k_{n}}
. As
ts_{i}.prom=\emptyset
, all program steps are certified and we get

\vec{T}_{1}^{0}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}^{1}\ldots op_{k_{1}}^{1}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau_{1}}\vec{T}_{1}^{k_{1}}\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}^{2}\ldots op_{k_{2}}^{2}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau_{2}}\ldots\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}^{n}\ldots op_{k_{n}}^{n}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}_{\tau_{n}}\vec{T}_{n}^{k_{n}}

Hence also
\langle\vec{T}_{0}^{p},M\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}^{*}\langle\vec{T},M\rangle
.

0.B.3
Completeness

Finally, we prove completeness of our proof calculus. We must prove that for every trace of a program we can construct local event structures, whereby the parallel composition of the local event structures contains an interference-free configuration. We construct these local event structures by induction following the execution trace.

See
5.2

For the proof, we need a number of well-formedness conditions on the event structures our proof rules construct. First of all, we slightly extend the action labels of events: instead of
\mathsf{rd}_{\tau}(x,\kappa)
we use
\mathsf{rd}_{\tau}^{t}(x,\kappa)
to denote the timestamp
t
of the read value in memory, and we similarly use
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}^{t}(x,\kappa)
.
In a trace
tr=\langle\vec{T}_{0},M_{0}\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\ldots\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{2}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle\vec{T},M\rangle
we define
\mathbb{T}_{rd}(tr,\tau)
to be the set of timestamps that thread
\tau

reads
from, similarly
\mathbb{T}_{\mathit{ff}}(tr,\tau)
for the timestamps of the fulfills.
With these extensions at hand, we say that an event structure
{\cal E}=(E,\twoheadrightarrow,\#,\Lambda,\ell)
of thread
\tau
is
timestamp closed
if the following holds:
\forall t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}_{rd}(tr,\tau),e\in E
, if
\ell(e)=\mathsf{rd}_{\cdot}^{t}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
t^{\prime}<t
,
t^{\prime}\neq 0
, then there exists an
e^{\prime}\in E
with
\ell(e^{\prime})\in\{\mathsf{rd}_{\cdot}^{t^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot),\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}^{t^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot)\}
;
{\cal E}
is
timestamp ordered
if
\forall t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}_{rd}(tr,\tau)\cup\mathbb{T}_{\mathit{ff}}(tr,\tau)
we have: if
t^{\prime}<t
, then
t^{\prime}
-labelled events cannot be flow-after
t
labelled events;

{\cal E}
contains a
modification order
for all locations
x
if for all
x\in\textsc{Loc}
,
\twoheadrightarrow^{+}|_{\mathit{Act}^{x}\times\mathit{Act}^{x}}
is a total order.
Finally, we need some conditions on the read events occuring in the event structures. Recall that the rule
PR-ReadNew
may introduce a number of read events (ordered in a chain) of which only the last in the chain is used for reading at that time. This reading is marked by attaching a bar event flow after the read. We define a read event
e
in
{\cal E}
to be
unbarred
,
ubr_{\cal E}(e)
, if
\ell(e)=\mathsf{rd}_{\cdot}(x,\cdot)\wedge\neg\exists e^{\prime}
s.t.
\ell(e^{\prime})=\mathsf{bar}(\cdot,x)\wedge e\twoheadrightarrow_{\cal E}e^{\prime}
. We say that all
read chains end in a bar
in
{\cal E}
if the following holds:

\forall e\in E:ubr_{\cal E}(e)\Rightarrow\exists m,\exists e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}\in\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}),e_{1}=e,e_{i}\twoheadrightarrow_{\cal E}e_{i+1}\wedge\neg ubr(e_{m})
.

Proof

Assume
tr:\langle\vec{T_{0}},M_{0}\rangle\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{1}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle\vec{T_{1}},M_{1}\rangle\ldots\mathrel{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\mathrel{-}_{\vphantom{{}}}\mkern-8.0mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle op_{m}}}{{\cleaders\hbox{$\smash{\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu}$}\hfill\vphantom{\lower 1.0pt\hbox{$\rightarrow$}}_{\vphantom{{}}}}}\mkern-11.0mu\rightarrow$}}\langle\vec{T_{m}},M_{m}\rangle=\langle\vec{T},M\rangle
is the trace such that all promises are done at the beginning, and let
op_{j}
be the last such promise operation leading to
\langle\vec{T}_{j},M_{j}\rangle
. Note first that
M_{j}=M_{j+1}=\ldots=M_{m}=M
(only promises change the memory). We now inductively construct the event structures
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
of every thread
k
,
1\leq k\leq n
(and by this the proof outlines) for all indizes
i=j
to
m
.
In every step, the event structures
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
are (i) timestamp closed, (ii) timestamp ordered, (iii) contain a modification order, (iv) all read chains end in a bar and moreover (v)
\langle\vec{T}_{i}(\tau_{k}),M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
using the mapping
\psi:\mathsf{Ff}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})\cup\mathsf{Rd}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})\cup\{e_{\mathsf{ini}}\}\rightarrow dom(M)
to be

\displaystyle e_{\mathsf{ini}}

\displaystyle\mapsto 0

\displaystyle\mathsf{rd}_{\cdot}^{t}(\cdot,\cdot)

\displaystyle\mapsto t

\displaystyle\mathsf{ff}_{\cdot}^{t}(\cdot,\cdot)

\displaystyle\mapsto t

Induction Start

i=j
:
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}=\mathsf{Ini}
. As the event structure
\mathsf{Ini}
contains just a single event, it is timestamp closed, timestamp ordered and contains a modification order. Moreover,
\langle\vec{T}_{j}(\tau_{1}),M\rangle\in\llbracket\mathsf{Ini}\rrbracket
because the event
e_{\mathsf{ini}}
gets mapped to 0 by
\psi
, and hence all views are 0, and the promise set contains all entries in
M
of
\tau_{1}
.

Induction Step

Assume
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
with properties (i) to (iv) has been constructed. We now have different cases depending on the next operation
op_{i+1}
. Let
\vec{T}_{i}(\tau_{k})=(S^{i},ts^{i})
.

1.

op_{i+1}
is not an operation of
\tau_{k}
:

Then
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}={\cal E}_{k}^{i}
. As the event structure is not changing and the state of thread
\tau_{k}
is not changing, all properties are trivially preserved.

2.

op_{i+1}=\textnormal{{dmb}}
is an operation of thread
\tau_{k}
:

Then
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}={\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus\mathsf{fnc}_{\tau_{k}}
.
As the new event is neither a read nor a fulfill (which would have timestamps), event structure
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
is still timestamp closed, timestamp ordered, contains a modification order and all read chains end in bar. As for the views: there are two views affected by the fence operation,
v_{read}
and
v_{wNew}
. We only consider these here. First,
v_{read}
. By the operational semantics, we know that
ts^{i+1}.v_{read}=ts^{i}.v_{read}\sqcup ts^{i}.v_{wOld}
. We need to show that this fits to the semantics of
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
given the semantics of
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
and operation
\oplus
.

\displaystyle ts^{i}.v_{read}\sqcup ts^{i}.v_{wOld}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})\big{)}\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})}\psi(e)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{Act}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{Act}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})}\psi(e)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{e\in\big{(}\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i+1})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}_{k}^{i+1})\big{)}\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i+1})}\psi(e)

The last term corresponds to
ts^{i+1}.v_{read}
when calculated from
{\cal E}_{1}^{i+1}
. For
v_{wNew}
we get almost the same reasoning as the new fence event is placed flow after all but test events.

3.

op_{i+1}=\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{k}}(x,\kappa)
is an operation of thread
\tau_{k}
:

By the operational semantics, this transition results from a statement
\textnormal{{store}}\,\,x\,\,a
for some register
a
such that
ts^{i}.regs(r)=\kappa@v_{a}
. Let
t
be the timestamp of the promise in
M
being fulfilled, i.e.
t\in ts^{i}.prom
.
Since
\langle\vec{T}_{i}(\tau_{k}),M_{i}\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
(by induction hypothesis), we get
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{{\cal E}_{k}^{i}}=\kappa
. Hence proof rule
Write
is applicable and we let
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}={\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus^{a}\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{k}}^{t}(x,\kappa)
. We show properties (i) to (v). By induction hypothesis,
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
is timestamp closed as the additional event is not a read event. It is timestamp ordered as the new event is added flow after all events
e^{\prime}
with
\ell(e^{\prime})\in\mathit{Act}^{x}
, and we know by the operational semantics that
ts^{i}.coh(x)<t
, and by
\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
that
ts^{i}.coh(x)
is the maximum of all fulfills and reads to
x
. It moreover maintains the existence of a modification order for
x
due to the same reasons. All read chains end in bar because no new reads are added.

Next to the state after the fulfill: the operational semantics changes the view
coh(x)
and
v_{wOld}
as well as
prom
and keeps the rest. We need to show that this after state is in
\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}\rrbracket
. First,
coh(x)=t
(by operational semantics) and this would be derived from
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
as the new event
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{k}}^{t}(x,\kappa)
is maximal on all
x
-labelled events.
Also, since this event is now in the event structure,
t
is not in
prom
anymore. Finally,
v_{wOld}
is derived from the event structure as the maximum of all timestamps of fulfills. This includes
t
and hence
v_{wOld}:=v_{wOld}\sqcup t
holds. For the remaining views:
v_{C}
is unchanged because no new test events are added;
v_{wNew}
is unchanged because no fence is added; all
v_{a}
’s are kept as no bar events are added and
v_{read}
is unchanged as neither fences nor bars are added.

4.

op_{i+1}=lst(a,exp)
is an operation of thread
\tau_{k}
:

Then
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}={\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,exp)
. As the new event is neither a read nor a fulfill,
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
is still timestamp closed, timestamp ordered, contains a modification order and all read chains end in bar. Further
\llbracket exp\rrbracket_{\cal E}=\llbracket exp\rrbracket_{ts^{i+1}.regs}@v
for some view
v
and
ts^{i+1}.v_{a}=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prBar}_{a}({\cal E}_{k}^{i+1})}\psi(e)
. Both equations follow directly from the construction in the proof of Proposition
6
.

5.

op_{i+1}=asm(\beta)
is an operation of thread
\tau_{k}
:

Since
\langle\vec{T}_{i}(\tau_{k}),M_{i}\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
and
\llbracket\beta\rrbracket_{ts^{i}.regs}=\mathsf{true}@v
, for some view
v
, we get
\llbracket\beta\rrbracket_{{\cal E}_{k}^{i}}=\mathsf{true}
. Hence proof rule
Assume
is applicable and we let
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}={\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus\mathsf{tst}_{\tau_{k}}(\beta)
. As the new event is neither a read nor a fulfill,
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
is still timestamp closed, timestamp ordered, contains a modification order and all read chains end in bar. Now we only need to show that
ts^{i+1}.v_{C}=\bigsqcup_{e\in\mathit{prTst}_{\tau_{k}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i+1})}\psi(e)
which follows directly from the construction in the proof of Proposition
7
.

6.

op_{i+1}=rd(x,\kappa)
is a transition of thread
\tau_{k}
executing instruction
a:=\textnormal{{load}}\,x
thereby reading from timestamp
t
:

Let
T=\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{q}\}
be the set of timestamps that thread
\tau_{k}
will read in
tr

after and including

op_{i+1}

for which there is furthermore no timestamp in
\mathbb{T}_{\mathit{ff}}(tr,\tau_{k})
.
Let
T_{\leq t}=\{t^{\prime}\in T\mid t^{\prime}\leq t\}
and let
t^{1}<t^{2}<\ldots<t^{p}
be an ordering of that set. Note that
t^{p}=t
.
By timestamp closedness, there is some index
\ell
,
0\leq\ell\leq p
, such that all timestamps
t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}_{rd}(tr,\tau_{k})
with
t^{\prime}\leq t^{\ell}
have read or fulfill events in
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
.
Two cases need to be considered:

If
\ell=p
, i.e. read or fulfill events for all
T_{\leq t}
are in
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
, we apply rule
PR-ReadEx
.
We let
e^{\prime}=last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})
. By existence of a modification order,
e^{\prime}
is uniquely defined. We need to show that the timestamp of
e^{\prime}
is
t
. Assume the contrary, i.e.
e^{\prime}
has a timestamp
t^{\prime}\neq t
. Let
e
be the event in
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
with timestamp
t
.
e
is also an event on location
x
, i.e. by modification order and
e^{\prime}\in last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})
, we have to have
e\twoheadrightarrow^{+}e^{\prime}
. By timestamp ordering, we thus have
t<t^{\prime}
. By the operational semantics, we get
t^{\prime}>ts^{i}.v_{read}\sqcup ts^{i}.coh(x)
. As
\langle ts^{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
,
e^{\prime}
has to be an unbarred read (because in case of a fulfill,
ts^{i}.coh(x)\geq t^{\prime}
; in case of a barred read
ts^{i}.v_{read}\geq t^{\prime}
).
However, in
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
all read chains end in a bar, hence
ts^{i}.v_{read}
is greater or equal the timestamp of the last event in the chain, and hence greater or equal
t^{\prime}
. Contradiction. Hence the timestamp of
last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})
is
t
.

We now let
e=last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E}_{k}^{i})
to match the notation of the rule.
As
\langle ts^{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
using the specific
\psi
and
M(t)
being
\langle x:=\kappa\rangle_{\tau}
,
e
is labelled

\mathsf{rd}_{\tau}^{t}(x,\kappa)
,
\mathsf{ff}_{\tau}^{t}(x,\kappa)
or
\mathsf{ini}
(with
\kappa=0
). We then let
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}=rstr_{e}^{x}({\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,x))
.
We need to show this new event structure to satisfy all properties. First, as we neither add new read nor new fulfill events,
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
is still timestamp closed, timestamp ordered and contains a modification order. As we only add additional bar events, all read chains continue to end in a bar.
We need to show
\langle ts^{i+1},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}\rrbracket
. The reasoning for promises and views follows that used in the proof of Proposition
3
. Finally, we need to take a look at the new location restriction in
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
. By the operational semantics, we get

\forall t^{\prime},t<t^{\prime}\leq ts^{i}:v_{read}\sqcup ts^{i}.coh(x)\Rightarrow M(t^{\prime})\neq x

By
\langle ts^{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
, we thus get

\forall t^{\prime},t<t^{\prime}\leq\bigsqcup_{f\in(\mathit{prFnc}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{i}_{k})\cap\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{i}_{k}))\cup\mathit{prBar}_{\tau}({\cal E}^{i}_{k})\cup\mathsf{Ff}_{\tau}^{x}({\cal E}_{K}^{i})}\psi(f)\quad\Rightarrow M(t^{\prime})\neq x

Hence
M
satisfies the additional memory constraint placed by
restr_{e}^{x}({\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,x))
.

Second case. If
\ell<p
, we apply rule
PR-ReadNew
and let
{\cal E}^{\prime}
be the event structure with
E^{\prime}=\{e^{\ell+1},\ldots,e^{p}\}
,
e^{\ell+1}\twoheadrightarrow^{\prime}\ldots\twoheadrightarrow^{\prime}e^{p}
,
\ell^{\prime}(e^{m})=\mathsf{rd}_{\tau}^{t^{m}}(x,u)
such that
M(t^{m})=\langle x:=u\rangle_{\tau}
. By construction,
{\cal E}^{\prime}
is sequential,
\ell^{\prime}(E^{\prime})\subseteq\mathit{Act}^{Rd}
,
last_{\mathit{Act}^{x}}({\cal E}^{\prime})=e
is an event labelled
\mathsf{rd}_{\tau}^{t}(x,\kappa)
and hence rule
PR-ReadNew
is applicable. We now let
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}=({\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus{\cal E}^{\prime})\oplus\mathsf{bar}(a,x)
.
Next to the properties. In
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
all read chains end in a bar (as the additional read chain ends in
e
followed by
\mathsf{bar}(a,x)
;
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
contains a modification order (as
{\cal E}^{\prime}
is sequential and hence contains a modification order,
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
contains a modification order and
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\oplus{\cal E}^{\prime}
orders all events on same locations.
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
is timestamp ordered:
{\cal E}^{\prime}
is timestamp ordered by construction,
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
is timestamp ordered by induction, now assume that here is some event
f\in{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
and
g\in{\cal E}^{\prime}
with timestamps
t^{f}
and
t^{g}
such that
f\twoheadrightarrow g
but
t^{g}>t^{f}
. By
\oplus
on event structures, there is some location
y
such that
f
is a fulfill on
y
and
g
a read of
y
. As
\langle ts^{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
,
ts^{i}.coh(y)\geq t^{f}
. However, then the trace
tr
cannot later have a read of
y
from a timestamp earlier than
t^{f}
(by view monotonicity and operational semantics). Hence
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
is timestamp ordered. Timestamp closedness follows by construction of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
which assumes there are already events in
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
for all timestamps
t^{\prime}\leq t^{\ell}
.

It remains to be shown that
\langle ts^{i+1},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}\rrbracket
.
Note first that
t
is larger than all timestamps of all events in
{\cal E}_{k}^{i}
. As
\langle ts^{i},M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}^{i}\rrbracket
, we thus have

ts^{i}.v_{read}<t\wedge ts^{i}.v_{a}<t\wedge\forall y\in\textsc{Loc}:ts^{i}.coh(y)<t

Hence
ts^{i+1}.v_{read}=ts^{i+1}.v_{a}=ts^{i+1}.coh(x)=t
. This is consistent with
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
because
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
has a bar event directly flow after the maximal read event
e^{p}
in
{\cal E}^{\prime}
which has timestamp
t
. Views
v_{C},v_{wOld}
,
v_{wNew}
,
coh(y)
for
y\neq x
do not change as neither new test nor fulfill nor fence nor
\mathsf{bar}(\cdot,y)
events are added. Finally,
ts^{i+1}.prom=ts^{i}.prom
and this still matches
{\cal E}_{k}^{i+1}
as no fulfill events are added.

We next let
{\cal E}_{k}={\cal E}_{k}^{m}
,
1\leq k\leq n
. We need to show that
{\cal E}_{1}
to
{\cal E}_{n}
are synchronisable and that there exists some interference free configuration
C\in({\cal E}_{1}||\ldots||{\cal E}_{n})
.

{\cal E}_{1}
to
{\cal E}_{n}
are synchronisable because the read events in the event structure correspond to the read operations occurring in the execution, they read from memory and every entry in memory has a fulfill operation in the execution (as all promise sets of threads are empty at the end) and hence a fulfill event in the event structures.

We construct the configuration
C
, i.e. a set of events. Note that we here mix events and their labellings.

1.

(e_{\mathsf{ini}},\ldots,e_{\mathsf{ini}})\in E_{C}
,

2.

Let
t\in dom(M)\setminus\{0\}
.
For every
t
we construct a single event
e_{t}=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})
as follows:

(i)
e_{k}=*
if
t\notin\mathbb{T}_{rd}(\tau_{k})\cup\mathbb{T}_{\mathit{ff}}(\tau_{k})
.

(ii)
e_{k}=\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{k}}^{t}(x,\kappa)
if
t\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathit{ff}}(\tau_{k})
.

(iii)
e_{k}=\mathsf{rd}_{\tau_{i}}^{t}(x,\kappa)
if
t\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathit{rd}}(\tau_{k})
and
t\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathit{ff}}(\tau_{i})
else.

3.

for every
e_{k}\in{\cal E}_{k}
with
\ell_{k}(e_{k})\in\{\mathsf{fnc}_{\tau_{k}},\mathsf{tst}_{\tau_{k}}(\cdot),\mathsf{bar}(a,\cdot)\mid a\in R(\tau_{k})\}
, we add one event
(*,\ldots,e_{k},\ldots,*)
to
C
.

We first show that
C\in\mathit{Conf}({\cal E}_{1}||\ldots||{\cal E}_{k})
, i.e. it is a configuration.

•

C
is cycle-free:

By construction, all
{\cal E}_{k}
are cycle-free. So, cycles may only arise because of synchronization. Assume there are timestamps
t_{1},t_{2}
and events
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})
(labelled with
t_{1}
) and
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})
(labelled with
t_{2}
) such that
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})\twoheadrightarrow^{+}(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})
and
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\twoheadrightarrow^{+}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})
. This ordering has to come from some
{\cal E}_{k}
, hence contradicts timestamp orderedness of all
{\cal E}_{k}
.

•

C
is conflict free:

This holds because all
{\cal E}_{k}
are conflict-free and no event gets paired with two or more different events.

•

C
is left-closed up to conflicts. Let
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}),(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\in E_{{\cal E}_{1}||\ldots||{\cal E}_{n}}
such that we have
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})\twoheadrightarrow(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})
and
(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})\in C
, however,
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})\notin C
. Assume w.l.o.g. that the flow is coming from
d_{k}\twoheadrightarrow_{k}e_{k}
. By construction of
C
, the event
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})
has to have either (1)
\ell_{k}(d_{k})=\mathsf{rd}_{\tau_{i}}^{t}(\cdot,\cdot)
and there exists some
j
such that
d_{j}=*
but
t\in\mathbb{T}_{rd}(\tau_{j})
or
d_{i}=*
or (2)
\ell_{k}(d_{k})=\mathsf{ff}_{\tau_{k}}^{t}(\cdot,\cdot)
and exists some
j
such that
d_{j}=*
but
t\in\mathbb{T}_{rd}(\tau_{j})
(otherwise the construction of
C
would have included the event). In both cases
e_{t}\#(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})
and
e_{t}\twoheadrightarrow(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n})
guaranteeing left-closedness up to conflicts.

Next, we look at interference freedom. Note that by construction
C
is thread-covering. It contains no unsynchronised reads as the read events in the trace need to read from a promise in memory, hence there has to exist a fulfill.
For the ordering
\prec
we now take the ordering as induced by the timestamps on events.
By timestamp ordering we then get that
\twoheadrightarrow_{C}^{*}\cap(\mathit{Act}^{x}(E_{C})\times\mathit{Act}^{x}(E_{C}))\subseteq\prec
. Note furthermore that by construction
\langle\vec{T}(\tau_{k}),M\rangle\in\llbracket{\cal E}_{k}\rrbracket
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, using the mapping
\psi
as detailed above. Hence, the location restrictions are also met.
